  BE + TO VERB (asıdı², asıydı²) (future plans and arrangements, or to give instructions)
  
  Linda is to be transferred to my office next week (transfer olunasıdı gələn həftə)
  Jim, you are to be in charge untill İ get back (sən əlaqədar olacaqsan mən qayıdana qədər)
  
  Sophie was to deliver the notes at the meeting (notları iclasda verəsi idi)
  The troop were to be withdrawn from the border (dəstə geri çəkdiriləsi idi)
  Jim was to be in charge (Jim nəzarət edəsi idi)

      It’s basically the same structure with the present perfect ( have + v3 ). 
      
      "Were to have done" is a formal, hypothetical conditional structure used to emphasize the high improbability or imagined nature of a past action. It frequently appears in "if" clauses (e.g., If I were to have done that, I would have failed), often implying a past event was unrealistic or unlikely to happen.

      @mamedabd4627  "He was to speak at the conference."
      "He was going to speak at the conference."
      "He was supposed to speak at the conference."
      
      All three of the above are correct; the first has some added ambiguity, as it can also refer to something that did happen ("He was to speak at the conference every year until he retired in 1963"). Leaving that issue aside, the "was to" version is quite elegant, but it is probably less common in spoken US English than the other forms.

  How come? - Як так?

  Get it? - Розумієш?

  What's up? - Як справи?
  Who cares? - Кого це турбує. (Кому не пофіг?)
  Now what? - Що тепер?
  How so? - Як так?
  You're (who's) in? (Who's in?) - Ти (Хто) з нами?/Ти (Хто) в справі?

    ↳ **@SMASHEnglish [channel author]** (👍0 · 2021-08-12) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=l1Z7XiMeWiw&lc=UgxvfxXdX2rxM3CvX5B4AaABAg.9KhcTlQRilB9KhfljczFmt
      There is no difference in terms of grammar, or the value of the qualification.
      The difference is in the vocabulary and topics. For example, in the speaking exam you will be asked about school instead of work etc. But the preparation is the same.
